<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> For coordinate sweeps with near-uniform line laws and line sizes among powers of two in a suitable fixed interval, we prove a Schatten-moment bound after conditioning on any feasible collection of prescribed card trajectories. An analytic transfer to binary sweeps then shows that a fixed number of sweeps mixes the Thorp shuffle on $`2^d`$ cards in $`O(d)`$ physical steps, matching the order of the support lower bound.

- **claim-binary-sweep-contraction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-binary)): There are absolute constants $g>0$ and $d_0$ such that, for $d\ge d_0$ and every $\lambda\vdash 2^d$, $$\\|K_\lambda\\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le D_\lambda^{-g}.$$ The sign representation is annihilated. There is an absolute positive integer $w$ such that the law after $w$ independent sweeps converges to uniform in total variation as $d\to\infty$, uniformly over deterministic initial decks.
  - Explanation: The averaged binary sweep contracts by a power of irreducible dimension and annihilates the sign representation. An absolute number of independent sweeps approaches uniformity in total variation, uniformly over deterministic initial decks.
- **claim-support-obstruction** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-support)): For every integer $t\ge0$, $$\\|q_d^{*t}-U_N\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\ge1-\frac{2^{tN/2}}{N!}.$$ Consequently, $$t_{\rm mix}(d)\ge \left\lceil\frac2N\log_2\!\left(\frac{3N!}{4}\right)\right\rceil =2d-O(1).$$
  - Explanation: The available fair bits bound the support of the permutation law. Testing this support against the uniform law gives a total variation obstruction and a lower bound on physical mixing time.
- **claim-conditional-sweep-moment** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ac-main)): Thereare a power of two $R\ge2$, a positive integer $q$, and $z_*>0$ such that every grid and every family $\mathcal H$ of prescribed disjoint trajectories defined above satisfy, for every $z\in[0,z_*]$ and every $\lambda\vdash s-h$, $$\log T_\lambda^{\mathcal H}\ \le\ -c(s)F(\lambda)+e(s)h\log s-C(\mathcal H). \label{ac:eq:15}$$ Here a zero moment gives $-\infty$. Use parameters $$a=\frac1{100},\quad c_0=e_0=\frac a{100},\qquad c(s)=c_0+\frac1{\sqrt{\log s}},\qquad e(s)=e_0-\frac1{\sqrt{\log s}}.$$ The empty diagram has dimension one. If $h=s$, the remaining bijection is the unique empty bijection and its moment is one. The assertion also includes the one-dimensional trivial representation for every $h$.
  - Explanation: For the admitted product grids and small real line perturbations, every feasible prescribed trajectory family admits a residual moment bound. The bound retains the trajectory potential while balancing representation dimension against the allowance for prescribed cards.
- **claim-conditional-sparse-contraction** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-sparse)): For every $0<\theta<1$ and $K_0\ge1$, there are $\rho>0$ and $R_0$ such that, for each power of two $R\ge R_0$, one can choose $z_{\rm sp}(R)>0$ with the following property. For every allowed grid and prescribed family $\mathcal H$, suppose that the integer $k\ge1$ satisfies $$h\le K_0 k,\qquad h+k\le s^{1-\theta}, \label{ac:eq:5}$$ and let $\lambda\vdash M=s-h$ satisfy $\lambda_1=M-k$ or $\lambda'_1=M-k$. Then, for every $z\in[0,z_{\rm sp}(R)]$, $$\\|K_\lambda^{\mathcal H}\\|_{\rm op}\le s^{-\rho k}. \label{ac:eq:6}$$
  - Explanation: A representation close to a row or column contracts when the tracked cards and prescribed trajectories remain sparse relative to the grid. The perturbation interval is chosen after the line scale, while the contraction exponent is chosen uniformly before that scale.
- **claim-signed-density-domination** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-density)): Let $P_\alpha$ be the isotypic projection for $\alpha\vdash l$ in this signed tensor representation. The types present are precisely those in the $(p,p)$-hook: all their boxes lie in the first $p$ rows or the first $p$ columns. For every such type there is a probability distribution $\mu_\alpha$ on even densities such that, in positive semidefinite order, $$P_\alpha\preceq \exp\{F(\alpha)+O(p^2\log(s+1))\} \mathbb E_{r\sim\mu_\alpha}r^{\otimes l}. \label{ac:eq:13}$$ Its multiplicity is at most $\exp(O(p^2\log(s+1)))$, and the number of types present is at most $(s+1)^{2p}$. All constants are absolute.
  - Explanation: The signed tensor action contains exactly the hook types. Each hook projection is dominated by a probability mixture of tensor powers of even densities, with dimension-dependent cost and controlled multiplicities and type counts.
- **claim-junction-overlap** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-overlap)): For the row and column type lists on the free junction sites and the full hook type $\gamma$ defined above, $$\log\\|P_\beta^C P_\gamma P_\alpha^R\\|_{\rm op}^2 \le F_R+F_C-F(\gamma)+h' +O((s_1+s_2)p^2\log(s+1)). \label{ac:eq:19}$$ Here a zero norm has logarithm $-\infty$, and the constant is absolute.
  - Explanation: The overlap of the row, column and full hook projections is bounded using local representation dimensions, the full hook dimension and the missing sites. This estimate supplies the dimension cancellation in the conditional moment induction.

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Binary coordinate sweep** ([source](#pm-h-2)): A binary coordinate sweep acts on the slots $\{0,1\}^d$. In each coordinate direction it independently fixes or swaps the two cards on every parallel edge, with probability $1/2$ each, and visits the coordinates in order.
- **Physical Thorp step** ([source](#pm-h-2)): On binary position strings one physical Thorp shuffle is $$(x_1,x_2,\ldots,x_d)\longmapsto (x_2,\ldots,x_d,x_1+\xi_{x_2,\ldots,x_d}),$$ where addition is modulo two and the bits $\xi$ are independent and fair.
- **Total variation distance and mixing time** ([source](#pm-h-2)): We use $$\\|\mu-\nu\\|_{\mathrm{TV}} =\frac12\sum_{g\in S_N}\|\mu(g)-\nu(g)\|,\qquad t_{\rm mix}(d)=\inf\{t\in\mathbb Z_{\ge0}: \\|q_d^{*t}-U_N\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\le1/4\}.$$
- **Binary sweep representation average** ([source](#pm-h-2)): For the irreducible unitary representation $\rho_\lambda$ of $S_N$ indexed by $\lambda\vdash N$, put $$K_\lambda=\mathbb E_{g\sim B_N}\rho_\lambda(g), \qquad D_\lambda=\dim\rho_\lambda.$$
- **Unnormalized Schatten norm** ([source](#sec:prelim)): We use ordinary, unnormalized matrix traces: $\\|A\\|_p^p=\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\|A\|^p$, where $\|A\|=(A^*A)^{1/2}$.
- **Representation dimension functional** ([source](#sec:prelim)): For a partition $\lambda\vdash l$, write $[\lambda]$ for the irreducible representation of $S_l$, $D_\lambda$ for its dimension, and $F(\lambda)=\log D_\lambda$.
- **Ordered dyadic product grid** ([source](#sec:paths)): Let $\Omega=\Omega_1\times\cdots\times\Omega_b$ be an ordered product grid with $b\ge1$, where $m_j=\|\Omega_j\|$ is a power of two in $[R,R^2]$, and put $s=\|\Omega\|=\prod_jm_j$.
- **Perturbed line law** ([source](#ac:eq:2)): On a line of size $m$, use $$L_m(z)=(1-z)U_m+zB_m, \label{ac:eq:2}$$ where $U_m$ is uniform on the permutations of that line and $B_m$ is a binary sweep on its $\log_2m$ bits.
- **Prescribed trajectories and holes** ([source](#sec:paths)): We may therefore prescribe a family $\mathcal H$ of $h$ such paths. We require their slots to be distinct at every layer, including input and output. These occupied slots will be called holes.
- **Conditional residual representation average** ([source](#sec:paths)): For $\lambda\vdash M$, let $K_\lambda^{\mathcal H}$ be its average in $[\lambda]$, using any fixed identifications of the two slot sets as in Section
- **Falling-factorial line cost and trajectory potential** ([source](#sec:paths)): For an integer $0\le u\le m$, define the line cost $$\phi_m(u)=\log\frac{m^u}{(m)_u},\qquad (m)_u=m(m-1)\cdots(m-u+1),\qquad (m)_0=1,$$ and set $$C(\mathcal H)=\sum_{\text{stage lines }L}\phi_{\|L\|}(h_L).$$
- **Placement** ([source](#sec:paths)): For a nonnegative integer $t$, a placement of $t$ distinguishable cards is an injection from $[t]=\{1,\ldots,t\}$ into the available slots; for $t=0$ there is one empty placement.
- **Conditional Schatten moment** ([source](#sec:paths)): For a positive integer $q$ to be fixed, define the unnormalized Schatten moment $$T_\lambda^{\mathcal H}=\\|K_\lambda^{\mathcal H}\\|_{2q}^{2q}.$$
- **Alternating placement kernel** ([source](#ac:eq:7)): Define a kernel from input placements to output placements by $$Q(x,y)=s^{-k}\sum_{A\subseteq[k]}(-1)^{k-\|A\|}s^{\|A\|}p_A(x_A,y_A). \label{ac:eq:7}$$
- **Even density** ([source](#sec:density)): An *even density* is a positive semidefinite operator on $V$ of trace one that is block diagonal for $E\oplus O$.
- **Row and column dimension sums** ([source](#ac:eq:20)): Put $$F_R=\sum_iF(\alpha_i),\qquad F_C=\sum_jF(\beta_j).$$

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-binary-operator-contraction | \\|K_\lambda\\|_{\mathrm{op}} | ≤ | $D_\lambda^{-g}$ | The exponent and dimension threshold are absolute existential constants. |
| q-physical-support-distance | \\|q_d^{*t}-U_N\\|_{\mathrm{TV}} | ≥ | $1-\frac{2^{tN/2}}{N!}$ | The support bound applies at every nonnegative integer time. |
| q-physical-mixing-lower-bound | t_{\rm mix}(d) | ≥ | $\left\lceil\frac2N\log_2\!\left(\frac{3N!}{4}\right)\right\rceil$ | The integer lower bound is explicit; its asymptotic form has a bounded additive error. |
| q-line-comparison-upper | L_m(z) | ≤ | $2U_m$ | The comparison interval depends on the fixed admitted line scale. |
| q-trajectory-potential-upper | C(\mathcal H) | ≤ | $bh$ | The allowance scales with the coordinate count and prescribed-card count. |
| q-main-scale-exponent | a | = | $\frac1{100}$ | An absolute fixed parameter. |
| q-main-base-coefficients | c_0=e_0 | = | $\frac a{100}$ | The dimension and trajectory base coefficients are equal. |
| q-main-dimension-coefficient | c(s) | = | $c_0+\frac1{\sqrt{\log s}}$ | The coefficient depends on the total grid size. |
| q-main-trajectory-coefficient | e(s) | = | $e_0-\frac1{\sqrt{\log s}}$ | The coefficient depends on the total grid size, with positivity ensured by the chosen line scale. |
| q-sparse-operator-contraction | \\|K_\lambda^{\mathcal H}\\|_{\rm op} | ≤ | $s^{-\rho k}$ | The exponent is uniform before the line scale is fixed; the perturbation interval may depend on that scale. |
| q-hook-type-count | number of types present | ≤ | $(s+1)^{2p}$ | The count is uniform over the tensor length allowed by the grid size. |
| q-hook-cutoff | p | = | $\lfloor s^a\rfloor$ | The cutoff grows with grid size using the fixed scale exponent. |
| q-binary-transfer-exponent | g | = | $a_0G(1)$ | The exponent is positive and independent of the grid and representation; no numerical value is specified. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-support-obstruction** [claim](#stmt-prop-support) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-support): Count the permutations determined by the fair bits and test their support against the uniform law. -> The support obstruction and physical mixing-time lower bound.
- **step-trajectory-potential** [claim](#stmt-ac-main) / [proof](#ac:eq:4): Factor conditioning by stage lines and express each falling-factorial ratio as an increment of the trajectory potential. -> A placement transition bound retaining the difference between augmented and original potentials.
- **step-sparse-alternation** [claim](#stmt-lem-sparse) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sparse): Project an alternating placement kernel onto the sparse type. Use rooted forests when many coordinates make shared lines rare, and grouped inverse-gamma moments when the coordinate count is bounded. -> Row-type contraction, with column types handled by sign cancellation.
- **step-sparse-common-interval** [claim](#stmt-lem-sparse) / [proof](#pm-h-9): Fix the contraction exponent before the line scale, then use strict slack, finiteness and continuity to obtain a common perturbation interval. -> Sparse contraction for all admitted grids on a common small real interval.
- **step-signed-density-domination** [claim](#stmt-lem-density) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-density): Decompose by parity and ordinary Schur–Weyl sectors, identify hook support through Pieri's rule, and average positive highest-weight densities over unitary groups. -> Positive tensor-density domination, the multiplicity bound and the hook-type count.
- **step-junction-overlap** [claim](#stmt-lem-overlap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-overlap): Dominate row and column projections by density mixtures, regularize the average row density, and apply the arithmetic-geometric mean inequality to retained sites. -> The junction overlap bound with its full-dimension saving and missing-site allowance.
- **step-conditional-induction-base-and-sparse** [claim](#stmt-ac-main) / [proof](#sec:induction): Choose constants in the required dependency order. Handle zero-dimensional logarithmic dimension by contraction, obtain the finite base case by continuity, and apply sparse contraction when the dimension allowance is insufficient. -> The conditional theorem for the base case, one-dimensional types and the sparse-dimension range.
- **step-uniform-hook-estimate** [claim](#stmt-ac-main) / [proof](#ac:eq:21): Split the coordinate list into row and column sweeps. Combine the child conditional moments with the junction overlap to cancel local dimension costs while preserving the full trajectory potential. -> A strengthened hook moment estimate uniform over prescribed families, without a lower dimension restriction.
- **step-restore-outside-hook** [claim](#stmt-ac-main) / [proof](#ac:part:245): Realize induction from the hook as placement fibers, expand a cyclic moment trace, cancel augmented potentials through the placement probability bound, and sum using stochasticity and Hölder's inequality. -> The full conditional moment estimate after the dimension reserve absorbs restoration costs.
- **step-analytic-binary-transfer** [claim](#stmt-thm-binary) / [proof](#sec:analytic): Remove the prescribed paths, bound the polynomial sweep operator on a small real interval and a larger complex disk, then apply a logarithmic barrier and the subharmonic maximum principle. -> Dimension-power operator contraction for the binary sweep.
- **step-fourier-mixing** [claim](#stmt-thm-binary) / [proof](#sec:analytic): Group consecutive bits into admitted coordinates, cancel the sign coefficient, and sum the Fourier bound using partition dimension estimates. -> An absolute number of sweeps approaches uniformity, yielding the physical upper bound matching the support lower bound in order.

```json
          "exact_quote": "The difference of potentials is the part of this estimate that permits further paths to be revealed during the induction.",
          "source_id": "PM-238.11"
        }
      ],

        "Near-uniform line comparison",
        "Layer-disjoint prescribed trajectories"

      "evidence": [
        {

          "exact_quote": "Projection to type $\\lambda$ therefore kills every term for a proper subset.",
          "source_id": "PM-238.11"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "ac:eq:10",
          "exact_quote": "Every path using a light line uses the same variable $W_L$. We will group all factors by line before taking moments; independence is available between lines, not between particle factors.",
          "source_id": "PM-238.11"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "pm-h-9",
          "exact_quote": "It is therefore enough to find one line whose residual sign has mean zero.",

      "output": "Row-type contraction, with column types handled by sign cancellation.",

      ],

    },

          "source_id": "PM-238.11"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "ac:eq:14",

          "source_id": "PM-238.11"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-overlap",

          "source_id": "PM-238.11"

      ],

      "output": "The junction overlap bound with its full-dimension saving and missing-site allowance.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-overlap"
    },
    {
```

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-binary-dimension-contraction**: The unconditioned binary sweep has dimension-dependent operator contraction in sufficiently large dimension, annihilates the sign representation, and mixes asymptotically after an absolute number of independent sweeps.
- **invariant-conditioned-trajectory-moment**: For admitted grids and feasible prescribed trajectories, the conditional Schatten moment retains a representation-dimension saving, a prescribed-path allowance, and the negative trajectory potential.
- **invariant-support-obstruction**: The support generated by the independent fair bits gives a total variation lower bound and an obstruction to mixing with an insufficient physical-step budget.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-use-arbitrary-deck-size**: Apply the binary contraction and physical-step mixing conclusion to an arbitrary deck size. -> The binary slot representation and coordinate rotation equivalence are specified for dyadic decks; an arbitrary deck has no supplied reduction preserving the stated law.
- **anti-use-conditioned-binary-transfer**: Use the analytic transfer to assert the conditional moment theorem for prescribed paths under binary line laws. -> The complex polynomial argument explicitly uses the unconditioned sweep. It supplies no transfer of the conditioned operators or their conditioning denominators.
- **anti-use-infeasible-path-conditioning**: Condition on endpoint assignments whose forced trajectories collide at an intermediate layer. -> Such assignments do not define an admitted disjoint path family, so the stated positive-probability conditioning construction does not apply.
- **anti-use-insufficient-support-budget**: Certify near-uniform full permutations using a physical-step budget that violates the support lower bound. -> The available fair-bit outcomes cannot give sufficient uniform mass to the resulting permutation support.
- **anti-use-explicit-universal-sweep-count**: Treat the asymptotic mixing theorem as an explicit numerical sweep budget for a particular finite deck. -> The theorem asserts absolute constants and convergence in growing dimension without supplying numerical values for the sweep count or dimension threshold.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main result is a uniform asymptotic mixing theorem with existential constants, proved through conditional moment induction and a subharmonic maximum principle. The source states: “There is an absolute positive integer $w$ such that the law after $w$ independent sweeps converges to uniform in total variation as $d\to\infty$, uniformly over deterministic initial decks.” Finite enumeration could check selected shuffle instances or the support obstruction, but would not decide this uniform conclusion or construct its unspecified constants.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The explicitly defined binary coordinate sweep can be implemented as a random permutation routine on binary indexed positions. The convergence theorem supports asymptotic mixing, but does not specify a numerical sweep budget for a chosen deck. |
| games_simulation | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | A card simulation can reproduce the physical shuffle by pairing cards from equal deck halves, randomly ordering each pair, and interleaving. The paper identifies how repeated physical steps correspond to coordinate sweeps. |
| tools | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The endpoint rule yields a concrete validator for prescribed trajectory families. It reconstructs intermediate slots and rejects collisions before conditional probabilities are considered. |
| algorithms | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The binary sweep is an explicit randomized permutation construction, and the support obstruction supplies a necessary mixing condition. These can guide algorithm selection without supplying the existential upper bound constants. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Placements and Young diagrams serve as mathematical indexing objects for transition operators and representation arguments. The paper does not construct a data structure with supported update or query guarantees. |
| hardware_accelerators | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | Parallel edge switches provide a concrete staged permutation architecture. Applying the mathematical guarantee requires an implementation whose stage ordering and randomness reproduce the binary sweep law. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper bounds statistical mixing and conditional representation moments, but supplies no cryptographic adversary model, keyed construction, or security reduction. Related enciphering work in the discussion does not establish those contracts here. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Tensor densities are introduced to dominate signed representation projections. No learning objective, training procedure, or prediction construction is given. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The junction argument bounds projection overlap on a fixed free site board. It does not optimize routing assignments, schedules, capacities, or an operational objective. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Conditional independence of row and column randomizations supports a mathematical factorization. The source provides no communication protocol, failure handling, or consistency mechanism for distributed execution. |
| other (Probability: exact support obstruction diagnostics) | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The support argument gives a finite diagnostic that can rule out a proposed mixing budget using the number of available random bit strings and the size of the permutation group. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-dyadic-binary-model** (hypothesis): The binary contraction and its physical mixing consequence concern dyadic decks with ordered independent fair coordinate switches. Arbitrary deck sizes, biased switches and correlated switch choices require separate arguments. Blocked use: ['Apply the binary mixing theorem to an arbitrary deck size.', 'Preserve the guarantee after replacing the specified switch randomness.']
- **limit-admitted-grid-scales** (hypothesis): The conditional theorem requires an ordered product grid whose coordinate sizes are powers of two in the fixed admitted interval. It does not assert the same estimate for arbitrary coordinate sizes or unrestricted aspect ratios. Blocked use: ['Apply the conditional moment estimate to a grid outside the admitted coordinate-size interval.']
- **limit-layer-disjoint-prescriptions** (hypothesis): Prescribed endpoints must force trajectories occupying distinct slots at every layer. Distinct endpoints alone do not establish admissible conditioning. Blocked use: ['Condition on prescribed trajectories that collide at an intermediate layer.', 'Validate feasibility using only the input and output layers.']
- **limit-small-real-conditional-interval** (hypothesis): The conditional moment theorem applies on its stated small real perturbation interval. A conditional guarantee outside that interval is ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Extend the conditional estimate to arbitrary real perturbations.', 'Infer conditional binary contraction from the small real interval alone.']
- **limit-positive-conditioning-denominators** (hypothesis): Residual line laws are obtained by conditioning on positive-probability prescribed assignments. The placement estimate applies to added paths through feasible augmented families; zero-probability prescriptions do not define such conditional laws. Blocked use: ['Form a residual conditional law by dividing by a zero conditioning probability.', 'Assign augmented path potentials to an infeasible placement transition.']
- **limit-sparse-representation-regime** (hypothesis): Sparse contraction requires the stated bounds relating tracked cards, prescribed cards and grid size, together with a representation close to a row or column. It is not a standalone estimate for arbitrary partitions or dense tracked families. Blocked use: ['Apply sparse contraction without verifying both sparsity inequalities.', 'Apply the sparse lemma to a partition lacking the stated row or column condition.']
- **limit-shared-line-moment-dependence** (hypothesis): The inverse-gamma argument groups factors sharing a stage line before taking moments. Independence holds between lines and cannot be substituted for independence between tracked particles sharing a line. Blocked use: ['Factor a joint moment into particle moments when those particles use the same line.']
- **limit-signed-hook-density-scope** (formal_scope): Density domination concerns hook types in the specified signed tensor representation and mixtures of even trace-one densities. It does not supply the same projection domination for arbitrary tensor actions or unrestricted diagram types. Blocked use: ['Use the hook density estimate for a type outside the signed tensor support.', 'Replace even densities by arbitrary matrices while retaining the domination argument.']
- **limit-fixed-junction-board** (formal_scope): The overlap estimate concerns the row and column projections on the fixed free junction board and the specified full hook type. It does not itself estimate trajectory probabilities or provide a routing optimization procedure. Blocked use: ['Treat projection overlap as a transition probability bound.', 'Use the overlap lemma as an algorithm for selecting optimal routes.']
- **limit-uniform-augmented-hook-estimate** (hypothesis): Restoring boxes outside the hook requires the hook estimate for every feasible augmented trajectory family, including hook types of small dimension. An estimate restricted to the original family or to large hook dimension cannot justify the placement-fiber argument. Blocked use: ['Reuse an unconditioned hook estimate inside conditioned placement fibers.', 'Impose a lower hook-dimension restriction during restoration.']
- **limit-retained-trajectory-potential** (formal_scope): The restoration argument cancels augmented trajectory potentials against placement probability increments and retains the original potential. Dropping that correction before the cyclic summation removes a required part of the proof. Blocked use: ['Replace the conditional placement bound by a bound that discards its potential increment.', 'Claim the same conditional induction after omitting the original trajectory correction.']
- **limit-unconditioned-analytic-transfer** (formal_scope): The complex polynomial and subharmonic argument is explicitly unconditioned. It does not transfer the conditional moment estimate to binary line laws with prescribed trajectories. Blocked use: ['Apply the complex disk argument directly to conditioned sweep operators.', 'Assert the conditional theorem at the binary parameter from the unconditioned transfer.']
- **limit-unspecified-uniform-constants** (nonconstructive): The proof selects scales and perturbation intervals through sufficiently large choices, finite continuity arguments and analytic barriers. Numerical values for the resulting universal contraction exponent, dimension threshold and sweep budget are ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Extract a numerical stopping rule for a specified finite deck from the existential theorem.', 'Treat finite instance agreement as verification of the uniform asymptotic conclusion.']
- **limit-asymptotic-approximate-uniformity** (approximation): The fixed sweep count yields convergence to uniform as dimension grows. This conclusion does not assert exact uniform sampling after a finite number of sweeps or supply a finite-deck error bound with evaluated constants. Blocked use: ['Advertise exact uniform sampling after the existential sweep count.', 'Assign a numerical total variation error to a particular finite deck using asymptotic convergence alone.']
- **limit-support-obstruction-is-necessary** (formal_scope): The support estimate is a lower bound on distance from uniform. Failure of that bound to exclude mixing is not evidence that mixing has occurred. Blocked use: ['Certify mixing merely because the support lower bound becomes uninformative.', 'Interpret adequate support cardinality as adequate probability balance.']
- **limit-empty-and-zero-moment-conventions** (edge_case): The theorem includes the empty residual bijection and trivial representations. A zero moment uses the stated negative-infinity convention; it must not be handled as a finite logarithm or confused with the moment of the empty bijection. Blocked use: ['Exclude fully prescribed families from the theorem without justification.', 'Evaluate a zero moment through an ordinary finite logarithm.', 'Replace the empty-bijection moment by a zero moment.']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 238 (`lean/docs/238.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Optimal-order mixing of the Thorp shuffle](../../preprints/Optimal-order-mixing-of-the-Thorp-shuffle-September-26-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [From partial permutation information to Fourier bounds](../../preprints/From-partial-permutation-information-to-Fourier-bounds-September-26-2026/main.pdf)
> - [Conditional permutations in a revealed switching environment](../../preprints/Conditional-permutations-in-a-revealed-switching-environment-September-26-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Routing densities and representation contraction for Thorp sweeps](../../preprints/Routing-densities-and-representation-contraction-for-Thorp-sweeps-September-26-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Row–column symmetry and contraction of coordinate sweeps](../../preprints/Row-column-symmetry-and-contraction-of-coordinate-sweeps-September-26-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Random-subspace tests and trace smoothing for coordinate sweeps](../../preprints/Random-subspace-tests-and-trace-smoothing-for-coordinate-sweeps-September-26-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Compatibility entropy and the spectrum of a Thorp sweep](../../preprints/Compatibility-entropy-and-the-spectrum-of-a-Thorp-sweep-September-26-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Signed tensor densities and diagram budgets for the Thorp shuffle](../../preprints/Signed-tensor-densities-and-diagram-budgets-for-coordinate-sweeps-September-26-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Conditional coordinate sweeps and analytic transfer](../../preprints/Conditional-coordinate-sweeps-and-analytic-transfer-September-26-2026/main.pdf)
> - [A strict four-row permanent inequality and permutation moments](../../preprints/A-strict-four-row-permanent-inequality-and-permutation-moments-September-26-2026/main.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalization proves optimal-order mixing of the Thorp shuffle on $2^d$ cards. After $1600d$ complete shuffles, the full permutation law converges in total variation to uniform as $d\to\infty$, uniformly over initial decks. Together with the support lower bound of $2d-O(1)$, this gives mixing time $\Theta(d)=\Theta(\log(2^d))$.
>
> The linked supporting results include frame and conditional-list estimates, regular trace and spectral bounds, signed moments, and full-density $L^2$ control. They also bound reciprocal Specht-dimension sums and give the eight-block Fourier estimate used in the mixing argument.
>
> The formalized supporting result turns bounds on partial-permutation cosets into Fourier bounds. Partition a finite set into $b\ge1$ nonempty blocks, and let $f\ge0$ be a subprobability weight on its symmetric group whose left-coset masses for each block subgroup are at most $B$. For every irreducible unitary representation of dimension $D$ and every $u>0$, both the squared Hilbert–Schmidt norm and squared operator norm of its Fourier transform are at most $bB\,C(u)D^{-1+(u+2)/b}$, where $C(u)$ is the stated symmetric-group degree constant.
>
> The paper's asymptotic conclusion about the product of two random permutations is outside this selected finite estimate.
>
> The formalized results give averaged conditional mixing for half-permutations in the revealed-path model and full-deck mixing for the Thorp shuffle. In particular, the full-deck total-variation distance tends to zero after $16040400d$ steps on $2^d$ cards, uniformly over deterministic initial decks. The conditional estimate is averaged over the actual outside-path law, rather than asserted for each individual environment. The formalization also includes the associated overlay, reset minorization, and two-color conditional constructions.
>
> The formalization covers nine routing and representation estimates for Thorp sweeps: adaptive operator and fourth-trace bounds, Casimir moments, dense truncation, sparse contact, harmonic-cycle contraction, smoothing, and three tail or sparse-saving estimates. They bound routing-density deviations and Fourier operators in dense and sparse regimes, including exponential savings in the level scale and representation dimension.
>
> The conditional high-height estimate requires a sufficiently large cutoff height $J$ that exceeds twice the number $s$ of coordinates in the fixed low block, so $J>2s$. These are the earlier nine statements associated with the paper; its changed later statements and six other result blocks are outside this scope.
>
> The formalization proves uniform representation bounds for one coordinate sweep of the Thorp shuffle. For each irreducible representation of dimension $D$, it chooses a weight between $D^{3/4}$ and $D$ and a Schatten exponent bounded by one absolute constant so that the weighted Schatten moment is at most one. The sweep's operator norm is consequently at most $D^{-c}$ for one absolute $c>0$.
>
> The supporting row–column estimate bounds the total squared overlap over all multiplicity copies on an occupied board, with explicit dependence on the row and column representation dimensions, the global dimension, the number of missing cells, and the number of rows or columns containing the global Young diagram. The paper's full physical-mixing conclusion is outside these selected representation estimates.
>
> The formalization proves a uniform trace-smoothing estimate for coordinate sweeps of the Thorp shuffle on $2^d$ cards. One absolute sweep parameter works for every $d\ge1$: the regular trace is at most $1+(2^d)^{-10}$, and the full permutation law after the corresponding fixed number of sweeps is within $\tfrac12(2^d)^{-5}$ of uniform in total variation, for every initial deck. Thus the selected upper bound uses $O(d)$ physical shuffles.
>
> The formalized result is the weighted compatibility theorem for balanced $A\times D$ rectangles, with $n=AD$ and $\sqrt n/2\le A,D\le2\sqrt n$. For arbitrary nonnegative weights on the row and column permutation groups, the normalized compatibility average is bounded by $\exp(C_0n^{54/100})$ times the product of the marginal $L^{1/\theta}$ factors, where $\theta=1-L/\log\sqrt n>0$ and the constants are absolute. Compatibility means injectivity in every original column. The bounded regular-moment and other moment/rank conclusions are not included.
>
> The formalization gives signed representation estimates for coordinate sweeps of the Thorp shuffle. For every signed occurrence of a Young diagram of size $2^d$, it bounds the logarithm of a fixed-order weighted sweep moment by a small multiple of the signed partition entropy plus an explicit remainder-size budget. The moment order is uniform over the diagrams and decompositions after the two small coefficients are fixed.
>
> The linked supporting angle bound controls the overlap of row, column, and global signed-type projections on an occupied rectangle, with explicit entropy, dimension, and missing-cell factors. These selected estimates support the paper's full-density argument; the full $L^2$ and total-variation mixing conclusion is outside them.
>
> The formalized binary-sweep theorem gives a universal power contraction in every irreducible representation: for sufficiently large $d$, the averaged sweep on $2^d$ slots has operator norm at most $D^{-g}$ in representation dimension $D$, for some $g>0$. The sign expectation is zero, and a fixed number of independent sweeps approaches uniform in total variation from every initial deck. A separate conditional moment estimate covers allowed power-of-two grids and disjoint coordinate-respecting trajectories, with its stated feasibility premise. Physical-shuffle law identification is not included.
>
> The formalization proves the paper's strict four-row permanent inequality. There are constants $4/3<p<2$ and $\varepsilon>0$ such that every probability law on $S_4$ within total-variation distance $\varepsilon$ of uniform, and with exactly uniform coordinate marginals, satisfies the permanent bound by the product of the four $L^p$ row norms for every nonnegative matrix. The exponent and neighborhood are uniform over those laws. The Thorp mixing consequence is outside this selected statement.

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

namespace CubeShuffle.Specht

open scoped BigOperators Classical

abbrev Cell (μ : YoungDiagram) := {x : ℕ × ℕ // x ∈ μ.cells}
def row {μ : YoungDiagram} (x : Cell μ) : ℕ := x.1.1
def col {μ : YoungDiagram} (x : Cell μ) : ℕ := x.1.2

lemma row_lt_colLen {μ : YoungDiagram} (x : Cell μ) : row x < μ.colLen (col x) :=
  YoungDiagram.mem_iff_lt_colLen.mp x.2
noncomputable def rowIndex {μ : YoungDiagram} (x : Cell μ) : Fin (μ.colLen 0) :=
  ⟨row x, (row_lt_colLen x).trans_le (μ.colLen_anti 0 (col x) (Nat.zero_le _))⟩

abbrev Tabloid (μ : YoungDiagram) :=
  {f : Cell μ → Fin (μ.colLen 0) // ∃ p : Equiv.Perm (Cell μ), f = rowIndex ∘ p}

noncomputable instance (μ : YoungDiagram) : Fintype (Tabloid μ) := Fintype.ofFinite _

noncomputable def baseTabloid (μ : YoungDiagram) : Tabloid μ := ⟨rowIndex, 1, rfl⟩

noncomputable def tabloidAct {μ : YoungDiagram} (p : Equiv.Perm (Cell μ)) :
    Equiv.Perm (Tabloid μ) where
  toFun f := ⟨fun x => f.1 (p⁻¹ x), by
    obtain ⟨q,hq⟩ := f.2
    refine ⟨q*p⁻¹, ?_⟩
    funext x
    simp only [hq,Function.comp_apply,Equiv.Perm.mul_apply]⟩
  invFun f := ⟨fun x => f.1 (p x), by
    obtain ⟨q,hq⟩ := f.2
    refine ⟨q*p, ?_⟩
    funext x
    simp only [hq,Function.comp_apply,Equiv.Perm.mul_apply]⟩
  left_inv f := by apply Subtype.ext; funext x; simp
  right_inv f := by apply Subtype.ext; funext x; simp

@[simp] lemma tabloidAct_apply {μ : YoungDiagram} (p : Equiv.Perm (Cell μ))
    (f : Tabloid μ) (x : Cell μ) : (tabloidAct p f).1 x = f.1 (p⁻¹ x) := rfl

@[simp] lemma tabloidAct_one (μ : YoungDiagram) : tabloidAct (1 : Equiv.Perm (Cell μ)) = 1 := by
  ext f x
  rfl

@[simp] lemma tabloidAct_mul {μ : YoungDiagram} (p q : Equiv.Perm (Cell μ)) :
    tabloidAct (p*q) = tabloidAct p * tabloidAct q := by
  ext f x
  simp only [tabloidAct_apply, mul_inv_rev,Equiv.Perm.mul_apply]

@[simp] lemma tabloidAct_inv {μ : YoungDiagram} (p : Equiv.Perm (Cell μ)) :
    tabloidAct p⁻¹ = (tabloidAct p)⁻¹ := by
  ext f x
  simp [tabloidAct,Equiv.Perm.inv_def]

noncomputable def tabloidRep (μ : YoungDiagram) :
    Representation ℂ (Equiv.Perm (Cell μ)) (Tabloid μ → ℂ) where
  toFun p :=
    { toFun := fun v f => v (tabloidAct p⁻¹ f)
      map_add' := by intros; rfl
      map_smul' := by intros; rfl }
  map_one' := by ext v f; simp
  map_mul' p q := by
    ext v f
    simp only [mul_inv_rev,tabloidAct_mul,Equiv.Perm.mul_apply]
    rfl

@[simp] lemma tabloidRep_apply {μ : YoungDiagram} (p : Equiv.Perm (Cell μ))
    (v : Tabloid μ → ℂ) (f : Tabloid μ) : tabloidRep μ p v f = v (tabloidAct p⁻¹ f) := rfl

noncomputable def delta {μ : YoungDiagram} (f : Tabloid μ) : Tabloid μ → ℂ :=
  fun g => if g=f then 1 else 0

noncomputable def colGroup (μ : YoungDiagram) : Subgroup (Equiv.Perm (Cell μ)) where
  carrier := {p | ∀ x, col (p x) = col x}
  one_mem' := by intro x; rfl
  mul_mem' := by intro p q hp hq x; exact (hp (q x)).trans (hq x)
  inv_mem' := by intro p hp x; simpa using (hp (p⁻¹ x)).symm

noncomputable def permSign {α : Type*} [Fintype α] [DecidableEq α] (p : Equiv.Perm α) : ℂ :=
  ((Equiv.Perm.sign p : ℤ) : ℂ)
section Alternator
variable {α V : Type*} [Fintype α] [DecidableEq α] [AddCommGroup V] [Module ℂ V]

noncomputable def alternator (C : Subgroup (Equiv.Perm α))
    (ρ : Representation ℂ (Equiv.Perm α) V) : Module.End ℂ V := by
  classical
  exact ∑ c : C, permSign (c : Equiv.Perm α) • ρ c

end Alternator

noncomputable def polytabloid (μ : YoungDiagram) : Tabloid μ → ℂ :=
  alternator (colGroup μ) (tabloidRep μ) (delta (baseTabloid μ))

noncomputable def space (μ : YoungDiagram) : Submodule ℂ (Tabloid μ → ℂ) :=
  Submodule.span ℂ (Set.range (fun p : Equiv.Perm (Cell μ) => tabloidRep μ p (polytabloid μ)))

lemma orbit_mem_space (μ : YoungDiagram) (p : Equiv.Perm (Cell μ)) :
    tabloidRep μ p (polytabloid μ) ∈ space μ := Submodule.subset_span ⟨p,rfl⟩

lemma action_mem_space (μ : YoungDiagram) (p : Equiv.Perm (Cell μ))
    (v : Tabloid μ → ℂ) (hv : v ∈ space μ) : tabloidRep μ p v ∈ space μ := by
  induction hv using Submodule.span_induction with
  | mem v hv =>
    obtain ⟨q,rfl⟩ := hv
    change ((tabloidRep μ p)*(tabloidRep μ q)) (polytabloid μ) ∈ space μ
    rw [←map_mul]
    exact orbit_mem_space μ (p*q)
  | zero => simp
  | add v w hv hw ihv ihw => simpa using (space μ).add_mem ihv ihw
  | smul a v hv ih => simpa using (space μ).smul_mem a ih

noncomputable def subrepresentation (μ : YoungDiagram) : Subrepresentation (tabloidRep μ) :=
  ⟨space μ, fun p v hv => action_mem_space μ p v hv⟩

noncomputable def representation (μ : YoungDiagram) :
    Representation ℂ (Equiv.Perm (Cell μ)) (space μ) :=
  (subrepresentation μ).toRepresentation

end CubeShuffle.Specht

namespace CubeShuffle.PermutationHilbert

open scoped BigOperators ComplexConjugate Classical
open Complex

variable {X : Type*} [Fintype X]

abbrev H (X : Type*) [Fintype X] := EuclideanSpace ℂ X

end CubeShuffle.PermutationHilbert

namespace CubeShuffle.Specht

open scoped BigOperators Classical

noncomputable def hilbertEquiv (μ : YoungDiagram) :
    (Tabloid μ → ℂ) ≃ₗ[ℂ] PermutationHilbert.H (Tabloid μ) :=
  (WithLp.linearEquiv 2 ℂ (Tabloid μ → ℂ)).symm

noncomputable def hilbertSpace (μ : YoungDiagram) :
    Submodule ℂ (PermutationHilbert.H (Tabloid μ)) := (space μ).map (hilbertEquiv μ).toLinearMap

noncomputable def spaceHilbertEquiv (μ : YoungDiagram) : space μ ≃ₗ[ℂ] hilbertSpace μ :=
  (hilbertEquiv μ).submoduleMap (space μ)

noncomputable def unitaryRepresentation (μ : YoungDiagram) :
    Representation ℂ (Equiv.Perm (Cell μ)) (hilbertSpace μ) :=
  ((spaceHilbertEquiv μ).conjAlgEquiv ℂ).toMonoidHom.comp (representation μ)

variable {α ι : Type*} [Fintype α] [Fintype ι]

noncomputable def relabelledUnitary (μ : YoungDiagram) (e : α ≃ Cell μ) :
    Representation ℂ (Equiv.Perm α) (hilbertSpace μ) :=
  (unitaryRepresentation μ).comp e.permCongrHom.toMonoidHom

end CubeShuffle.Specht

namespace CubeShuffle

open scoped BigOperators
open Filter

abbrev Card (d : ℕ) := Fin d → Bool

def Coins : ℕ → Type
  | 0 => Unit
  | d + 1 => Card d → Bool

instance (d : ℕ) : Fintype (Coins d) := by
  cases d <;> dsimp [Coins] <;> infer_instance

instance (d : ℕ) : Nonempty (Coins d) := by
  cases d <;> dsimp [Coins] <;> infer_instance

def switchFun {d : ℕ} (ξ : Card d → Bool) (x : Card (d + 1)) : Card (d + 1) :=
  Fin.cons (Bool.xor (x 0) (ξ (Fin.tail x))) (Fin.tail x)

theorem switchFun_involutive {d : ℕ} (ξ : Card d → Bool) :
    Function.Involutive (switchFun ξ) := by
  intro x
  funext i
  refine Fin.cases ?_ (fun j => ?_) i
  · simp only [switchFun, Fin.cons_zero, Fin.tail_cons]
    cases x 0 <;> cases ξ (Fin.tail x) <;> rfl
  · simp [switchFun, Fin.tail]

def pairSwitch {d : ℕ} (ξ : Card d → Bool) : Equiv.Perm (Card (d + 1)) :=
  { toFun := switchFun ξ
    invFun := switchFun ξ
    left_inv := switchFun_involutive ξ
    right_inv := switchFun_involutive ξ }

def rotate (d : ℕ) : Equiv.Perm (Card d) where
  toFun x i := x (finRotate d i)
  invFun x i := x ((finRotate d).symm i)
  left_inv x := by funext i; simp only [Equiv.apply_symm_apply]
  right_inv x := by funext i; simp only [Equiv.symm_apply_apply]

def step : (d : ℕ) → Coins d → Equiv.Perm (Card d)
  | 0, _ => 1
  | d + 1, ξ => rotate (d + 1) * pairSwitch ξ

def run (d : ℕ) : (t : ℕ) → (Fin t → Coins d) → Equiv.Perm (Card d)
  | 0, _ => 1
  | t + 1, ω => step d (ω (Fin.last t)) * run d t (fun i => ω i.castSucc)

lemma card_positions (d : ℕ) : Fintype.card (Card d) = 2 ^ d := by
  simp [Card]

end CubeShuffle

namespace CubeShuffle.Specht
open scoped BigOperators Classical

@[simp] lemma card_cell (μ : YoungDiagram) : Fintype.card (Cell μ) = μ.card := by
  simp [Cell, YoungDiagram.card]

abbrev Shapes (N : ℕ) := {μ : YoungDiagram // μ.card = N}

noncomputable def labels {N : ℕ} (μ : Shapes N) : Cell μ.1 ≃ Fin N :=
  (Fintype.equivFin (Cell μ.1)).trans (finCongr ((card_cell μ.1).trans μ.2))

noncomputable def cardLabels (d : ℕ) (μ : Shapes (2^d)) : Card d ≃ Cell μ.1 :=
  ((Fintype.equivFin (Card d)).trans (finCongr (card_positions d))).trans (labels μ).symm

noncomputable def cardRepresentation (d : ℕ) (μ : Shapes (2^d)) :
    Representation ℂ (Equiv.Perm (Card d)) (hilbertSpace μ.1) :=
  relabelledUnitary μ.1 (cardLabels d μ)

end CubeShuffle.Specht

namespace CubeShuffle.UnitaryFinite
open scoped BigOperators ComplexConjugate Classical
variable {G : Type*} [Group G] [Fintype G]
variable {V : Type*} [NormedAddCommGroup V] [InnerProductSpace ℂ V] [FiniteDimensional ℂ V]

noncomputable def continuousRepresentation (ρ : Representation ℂ G V) : G →* (V →L[ℂ] V) where
  toFun g := (ρ g).toContinuousLinearMap
  map_one' := by ext v; simp
  map_mul' g h := by ext v; simp

end CubeShuffle.UnitaryFinite

run_cmd Lean.modifyEnv fun env => Lean.Meta.auxLemmasExt.setState env {}

namespace CubeShuffle.UnitaryFinite
open scoped BigOperators Classical
variable {G : Type*} [Group G] [Fintype G]
variable {V : Type*} [NormedAddCommGroup V] [InnerProductSpace ℂ V] [FiniteDimensional ℂ V]
variable {Ω Ξ : Type*} [Fintype Ω] [Fintype Ξ]

noncomputable def sampleOperator (ρ : Representation ℂ G V) (P : Ω → G) : V →L[ℂ] V :=
  (Fintype.card Ω:ℂ)⁻¹ • ∑ ω,continuousRepresentation ρ (P ω)

end CubeShuffle.UnitaryFinite

noncomputable section
open scoped BigOperators Classical

namespace CubeShuffle.WeightedSweep
open Specht UnitaryFinite

def dimension (μ : YoungDiagram) : ℝ := Module.finrank ℂ (space μ)
def fourierSweep (d : ℕ) (μ : Shapes (2^d)) :
    hilbertSpace μ.1 →L[ℂ] hilbertSpace μ.1 :=
  sampleOperator (V := hilbertSpace μ.1) (cardRepresentation d μ) (run d d)
def schattenMoment {V : Type*} [NormedAddCommGroup V] [InnerProductSpace ℂ V]
    [FiniteDimensional ℂ V] (A : V →L[ℂ] V) (p : ℝ) : ℝ :=
  ∑ i : Fin (Module.finrank ℂ V), (A.toLinearMap.singularValues i) ^ p

def schattenNorm {V : Type*} [NormedAddCommGroup V] [InnerProductSpace ℂ V]
    [FiniteDimensional ℂ V] (A : V →L[ℂ] V) (p : ℝ) : ℝ :=
  schattenMoment A p ^ (1 / p)

end CubeShuffle.WeightedSweep

end

noncomputable section
open scoped BigOperators Classical

namespace CubeShuffle.WeightedSweep
open Specht UnitaryFinite

theorem weighted_sweep_moments :
    ∃ (W : YoungDiagram → ℝ) (pStar : ℝ) (p : ℕ → ℝ),
      (∀ μ : YoungDiagram, 0 < W μ) ∧
      2 ≤ pStar ∧
      (∀ d : ℕ, 1 ≤ d → 2 ≤ p d ∧ p d ≤ pStar) ∧
      ∀ d : ℕ, 1 ≤ d → ∀ μ : Shapes (2 ^ d),
        dimension μ.1 ^ (3 / 4 : ℝ) ≤ W μ.1 ∧
        W μ.1 ≤ dimension μ.1 ∧
        W μ.1 *
          schattenNorm (V := hilbertSpace μ.1) (fourierSweep d μ) (p d) ^ (p d) ≤ 1 ∧
        ‖fourierSweep d μ‖ ≤ dimension μ.1 ^ (-3 / (4 * pStar)) := by
  sorry

end CubeShuffle.WeightedSweep

end

end OAI
```

```json
      "name": "tabloidAct_mul",
      "statement_lean": "@[simp] lemma tabloidAct_mul {μ : YoungDiagram} (p q : Equiv.Perm (Cell μ)) :\n    tabloidAct (p*q) = tabloidAct p * tabloidAct q := by\n  ext f x\n  simp only [tabloidAct_apply, mul_inv_rev,Equiv.Perm.mul_apply]"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "lemma",
      "name": "tabloidAct_inv",
      "statement_lean": "@[simp] lemma tabloidAct_inv {μ : YoungDiagram} (p : Equiv.Perm (Cell μ)) :\n    tabloidAct p⁻¹ = (tabloidAct p)⁻¹ := by\n  ext f x\n  simp [tabloidAct,Equiv.Perm.inv_def]"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "tabloidRep",
      "statement_lean": "noncomputable def tabloidRep (μ : YoungDiagram) :\n    Representation ℂ (Equiv.Perm (Cell μ)) (Tabloid μ → ℂ) where\n  toFun p :=\n    { toFun := fun v f => v (tabloidAct p⁻¹ f)\n      map_add' := by intros; rfl\n      map_smul' := by intros; rfl }\n  map_one' := by ext v f; simp\n  map_mul' p q := by\n    ext v f\n    simp only [mul_inv_rev,tabloidAct_mul,Equiv.Perm.mul_apply]\n    rfl"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,

    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "delta",
      "statement_lean": "noncomputable def delta {μ : YoungDiagram} (f : Tabloid μ) : Tabloid μ → ℂ :=\n  fun g => if g=f then 1 else 0"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",

      "statement_lean": "noncomputable def colGroup (μ : YoungDiagram) : Subgroup (Equiv.Perm (Cell μ)) where\n  carrier := {p | ∀ x, col (p x) = col x}\n  one_mem' := by intro x; rfl\n  mul_mem' := by intro p q hp hq x; exact (hp (q x)).trans (hq x)\n  inv_mem' := by intro p hp x; simpa using (hp (p⁻¹ x)).symm"
    },

      "docstring": null,

      "statement_lean": "noncomputable def permSign {α : Type*} [Fintype α] [DecidableEq α] (p : Equiv.Perm α) : ℂ :=\n  ((Equiv.Perm.sign p : ℤ) : ℂ)"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "alternator",
      "statement_lean": "noncomputable def alternator (C : Subgroup (Equiv.Perm α))\n    (ρ : Representation ℂ (Equiv.Perm α) V) : Module.End ℂ V := by\n  classical\n  exact ∑ c : C, permSign (c : Equiv.Perm α) • ρ c"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "polytabloid",
      "statement_lean": "noncomputable def polytabloid (μ : YoungDiagram) : Tabloid μ → ℂ :=\n  alternator (colGroup μ) (tabloidRep μ) (delta (baseTabloid μ))"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,

    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "lemma",
      "name": "orbit_mem_space",
      "statement_lean": "lemma orbit_mem_space (μ : YoungDiagram) (p : Equiv.Perm (Cell μ)) :\n    tabloidRep μ p (polytabloid μ) ∈ space μ := Submodule.subset_span ⟨p,rfl⟩"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "lemma",
      "name": "action_mem_space",
      "statement_lean": "lemma action_mem_space (μ : YoungDiagram) (p : Equiv.Perm (Cell μ))\n    (v : Tabloid μ → ℂ) (hv : v ∈ space μ) : tabloidRep μ p v ∈ space μ := by\n  induction hv using Submodule.span_induction with\n  | mem v hv =>\n    obtain ⟨q,rfl⟩ := hv\n    change ((tabloidRep μ p)*(tabloidRep μ q)) (polytabloid μ) ∈ space μ\n    rw [←map_mul]\n    exact orbit_mem_space μ (p*q)\n  | zero => simp\n  | add v w hv hw ihv ihw => simpa using (space μ).add_mem ihv ihw\n  | smul a v hv ih => simpa using (space μ).smul_mem a ih"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "subrepresentation",
      "statement_lean": "noncomputable def subrepresentation (μ : YoungDiagram) : Subrepresentation (tabloidRep μ) :=\n  ⟨space μ, fun p v hv => action_mem_space μ p v hv⟩"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,

    },

    },

      "docstring": null,

      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "switchFun",
      "statement_lean": "def switchFun {d : ℕ} (ξ : Card d → Bool) (x : Card (d + 1)) : Card (d + 1) :=\n  Fin.cons (Bool.xor (x 0) (ξ (Fin.tail x))) (Fin.tail x)"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "theorem",
      "name": "switchFun_involutive",
      "statement_lean": "theorem switchFun_involutive {d : ℕ} (ξ : Card d → Bool) :\n    Function.Involutive (switchFun ξ) := by\n  intro x\n  funext i\n  refine Fin.cases ?_ (fun j => ?_) i\n  · simp only [switchFun, Fin.cons_zero, Fin.tail_cons]\n    cases x 0 <;> cases ξ (Fin.tail x) <;> rfl\n  · simp [switchFun, Fin.tail]"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "pairSwitch",
      "statement_lean": "def pairSwitch {d : ℕ} (ξ : Card d → Bool) : Equiv.Perm (Card (d + 1)) :=\n  { toFun := switchFun ξ\n    invFun := switchFun ξ\n    left_inv := switchFun_involutive ξ\n    right_inv := switchFun_involutive ξ }"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "rotate",
      "statement_lean": "def rotate (d : ℕ) : Equiv.Perm (Card d) where\n  toFun x i := x (finRotate d i)\n  invFun x i := x ((finRotate d).symm i)\n  left_inv x := by funext i; simp only [Equiv.apply_symm_apply]\n  right_inv x := by funext i; simp only [Equiv.symm_apply_apply]"
    },

      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "step",
      "statement_lean": "def step : (d : ℕ) → Coins d → Equiv.Perm (Card d)\n  | 0, _ => 1\n  | d + 1, ξ => rotate (d + 1) * pairSwitch ξ"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "run",
      "statement_lean": "def run (d : ℕ) : (t : ℕ) → (Fin t → Coins d) → Equiv.Perm (Card d)\n  | 0, _ => 1\n  | t + 1, ω => step d (ω (Fin.last t)) * run d t (fun i => ω i.castSucc)"
    },

      "statement_lean": "theorem weighted_sweep_moments :\n    ∃ (W : YoungDiagram → ℝ) (pStar : ℝ) (p : ℕ → ℝ),\n      (∀ μ : YoungDiagram, 0 < W μ) ∧\n      2 ≤ pStar ∧\n      (∀ d : ℕ, 1 ≤ d → 2 ≤ p d ∧ p d ≤ pStar) ∧\n      ∀ d : ℕ, 1 ≤ d → ∀ μ : Shapes (2 ^ d),\n        dimension μ.1 ^ (3 / 4 : ℝ) ≤ W μ.1 ∧\n        W μ.1 ≤ dimension μ.1 ∧\n        W μ.1 *\n          schattenNorm (V := hilbertSpace μ.1) (fourierSweep d μ) (p d) ^ (p d) ≤ 1 ∧\n        ‖fourierSweep d μ‖ ≤ dimension μ.1 ^ (-3 / (4 * pStar)) := by\n  sorry"
    }

    "text": "The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):\n\n- [Optimal-order mixing of the Thorp shuffle](../../preprints/Optimal-order-mixing-of-the-Thorp-shuffle-September-26-2026/paper.pdf)\n- [From partial permutation information to Fourier bounds](../../preprints/From-partial-permutation-information-to-Fourier-bounds-September-26-2026/main.pdf)\n- [Conditional permutations in a revealed switching environment](../../preprints/Conditional-permutations-in-a-revealed-switching-environment-September-26-2026/paper.pdf)\n- [Routing densities and representation contraction for Thorp sweeps](../../preprints/Routing-densities-and-representation-contraction-for-Thorp-sweeps-September-26-2026/paper.pdf)\n- [Row–column symmetry and contraction of coordinate sweeps](../../preprints/Row-column-symmetry-and-contraction-of-coordinate-sweeps-September-26-2026/paper.pdf)\n- [Random-subspace tests and trace smoothing for coordinate sweeps](../../preprints/Random-subspace-tests-and-trace-smoothing-for-coordinate-sweeps-September-26-2026/paper.pdf)\n- [Compatibility entropy and the spectrum of a Thorp sweep](../../preprints/Compatibility-entropy-and-the-spectrum-of-a-Thorp-sweep-September-26-2026/paper.pdf)\n- [Signed tensor densities and diagram budgets for the Thorp shuffle](../../preprints/Signed-tensor-densities-and-diagram-budgets-for-coordinate-sweeps-September-26-2026/paper.pdf)\n- [Conditional coordinate sweeps and analytic transfer](../../preprints/Conditional-coordinate-sweeps-and-analytic-transfer-September-26-2026/main.pdf)\n- [A strict four-row permanent inequality and permutation moments](../../preprints/A-strict-four-row-permanent-inequality-and-permutation-moments-September-26-2026/main.pdf)\n\n## Scope\n\nThe formalization proves optimal-order mixing of the Thorp shuffle on $2^d$ cards. After $1600d$ complete shuffles, the full permutation law converges in total variation to uniform as $d\\to\\infty$, uniformly over initial decks. Together with the support lower bound of $2d-O(1)$, this gives mixing time $\\Theta(d)=\\Theta(\\log(2^d))$.\n\nThe linked supporting results include frame and conditional-list estimates, regular trace and spectral bounds, signed moments, and full-density $L^2$ control. They also bound reciprocal Specht-dimension sums and give the eight-block Fourier estimate used in the mixing argument.\n\nThe formalized supporting result turns bounds on partial-permutation cosets into Fourier bounds. Partition a finite set into $b\\ge1$ nonempty blocks, and let $f\\ge0$ be a subprobability weight on its symmetric group whose left-coset masses for each block subgroup are at most $B$. For every irreducible unitary representation of dimension $D$ and every $u>0$, both the squared Hilbert–Schmidt norm and squared operator norm of its Fourier transform are at most $bB\\,C(u)D^{-1+(u+2)/b}$, where $C(u)$ is the stated symmetric-group degree constant.\n\nThe paper's asymptotic conclusion about the product of two random permutations is outside this selected finite estimate.\n\nThe formalized results give averaged conditional mixing for half-permutations in the revealed-path model and full-deck mixing for the Thorp shuffle. In particular, the full-deck total-variation distance tends to zero after $16040400d$ steps on $2^d$ cards, uniformly over deterministic initial decks. The conditional estimate is averaged over the actual outside-path law, rather than asserted for each individual environment. The formalization also includes the associated overlay, reset minorization, and two-color conditional constructions.\n\nThe formalization covers nine routing and representation estimates for Thorp sweeps: adaptive operator and fourth-trace bounds, Casimir moments, dense truncation, sparse contact, harmonic-cycle contraction, smoothing, and three tail or sparse-saving estimates. They bound routing-density deviations and Fourier operators in dense and sparse regimes, including exponential savings in the level scale and representation dimension.\n\nThe conditional high-height estimate requires a sufficiently large cutoff height $J$ that exceeds twice the number $s$ of coordinates in the fixed low block, so $J>2s$. These are the earlier nine statements associated with the paper; its changed later statements and six other result blocks are outside this scope.\n\nThe formalization proves uniform representation bounds for one coordinate sweep of the Thorp shuffle. For each irreducible representation of dimension $D$, it chooses a weight between $D^{3/4}$ and $D$ and a Schatten exponent bounded by one absolute constant so that the weighted Schatten moment is at most one. The sweep's operator norm is consequently at most $D^{-c}$ for one absolute $c>0$.\n\nThe supporting row–column estimate bounds the total squared overlap over all multiplicity copies on an occupied board, with explicit dependence on the row and column representation dimensions, the global dimension, the number of missing cells, and the number of rows or columns containing the global Young diagram. The paper's full physical-mixing conclusion is outside these selected representation estimates.\n\nThe formalization proves a uniform trace-smoothing estimate for coordinate sweeps of the Thorp shuffle on $2^d$ cards. One absolute sweep parameter works for every $d\\ge1$: the regular trace is at most $1+(2^d)^{-10}$, and the full permutation law after the corresponding fixed number of sweeps is within $\\tfrac12(2^d)^{-5}$ of uniform in total variation, for every initial deck. Thus the selected upper bound uses $O(d)$ physical shuffles.\n\nThe formalized result is the weighted compatibility theorem for balanced $A\\times D$ rectangles, with $n=AD$ and $\\sqrt n/2\\le A,D\\le2\\sqrt n$. For arbitrary nonnegative weights on the row and column permutation groups, the normalized compatibility average is bounded by $\\exp(C_0n^{54/100})$ times the product of the marginal $L^{1/\\theta}$ factors, where $\\theta=1-L/\\log\\sqrt n>0$ and the constants are absolute. Compatibility means injectivity in every original column. The bounded regular-moment and other moment/rank conclusions are not included.\n\nThe formalization gives signed representation estimates for coordinate sweeps of the Thorp shuffle. For every signed occurrence of a Young diagram of size $2^d$, it bounds the logarithm of a fixed-order weighted sweep moment by a small multiple of the signed partition entropy plus an explicit remainder-size budget. The moment order is uniform over the diagrams and decompositions after the two small coefficients are fixed.\n\nThe linked supporting angle bound controls the overlap of row, column, and global signed-type projections on an occupied rectangle, with explicit entropy, dimension, and missing-cell factors. These selected estimates support the paper's full-density argument; the full $L^2$ and total-variation mixing conclusion is outside them.\n\nThe formalized binary-sweep theorem gives a universal power contraction in every irreducible representation: for sufficiently large $d$, the averaged sweep on $2^d$ slots has operator norm at most $D^{-g}$ in representation dimension $D$, for some $g>0$. The sign expectation is zero, and a fixed number of independent sweeps approaches uniform in total variation from every initial deck. A separate conditional moment estimate covers allowed power-of-two grids and disjoint coordinate-respecting trajectories, with its stated feasibility premise. Physical-shuffle law identification is not included.\n\nThe formalization proves the paper's strict four-row permanent inequality. There are constants $4/3<p<2$ and $\\varepsilon>0$ such that every probability law on $S_4$ within total-variation distance $\\varepsilon$ of uniform, and with exactly uniform coordinate marginals, satisfies the permanent bound by the product of the four $L^p$ row norms for every nonnegative matrix. The exponent and neighborhood are uniform over those laws. The Thorp mixing consequence is outside this selected statement."
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


For coordinate sweeps with near-uniform line laws and line sizes among powers of two in a suitable fixed interval, we prove a Schatten-moment bound after conditioning on any feasible collection of prescribed card trajectories. An analytic transfer to binary sweeps then shows that a fixed number of sweeps mixes the Thorp shuffle on $`2^d`$ cards in $`O(d)`$ physical steps, matching the order of the support lower bound.
